On the anticyclotomic Iwasawa theory of newforms at Eisenstein primes of semistable reduction Thanks: TK was supported by the Deutsche Forschungsgemeinschaft (DFG), Geschäftszeichen STO 299/18-1, AOBJ: 667349 and by the 2021 MSCA Postdoctoral Fellowship 01064790 – ExplicitRatPoints while working on this article.
Timo Keller, Mulun Yin

Abstract
. Let f f be a newform of weight k = 2 ​ r k=2r and level N N with trivial nebentypus. Let 𝔭 ∤ 2 ​ N \mathfrak{p}\nmid 2N be a maximal ideal of the ring of integers of the coefficient field of f f such that the self-dual twist of the mod- 𝔭 \mathfrak{p} Galois representation of f f is reducible with constituents φ , ψ \varphi,\psi . Denote a decomposition group over the rational prime p p below 𝔭 \mathfrak{p} by G p G_{p} . We remove the condition φ | G p ≠ 𝟏 , ω \varphi|_{G_{p}}\neq\mathbf{1},\omega from [ CGLS22 ] , and generalize their results to newforms of higher weights 2 ​ r 2r with r r being odd. As a consequence, we prove some Iwasawa Main Conjectures and get the p p -part of the strong BSD Conjecture for elliptic curves of analytic rank 0 0 or 1 1 over 𝐐 \mathbf{Q} in this setting. In particular, non-trivial p p -torsion is allowed in the Mordell–Weil group. Using Hida families, we also prove an Iwasawa Main Conjecture for newforms of weight 2 2 of multiplicative reduction at Eisenstein primes. In the above situations, we also get p p -converse to the theorems of Gross–Zagier–Kolyvagin. The p p -converse theorems have applications to Goldfeld’s conjecture in certain quadratic twist families of elliptic curves having a 3 3 -isogeny.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
11G40 (Primary) 11G05, 11G10, 14G10 (Secondary)

Introduction

0.1.
Statement of the main results.

Let
f\in S_{k}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
with
k=2r
be a newform with coefficient field
\mathbf{Q}(f)
and coefficient ring
\mathbf{Z}[f]
. Let
F
be a finite extension of the completion of
\mathbf{Q}(f)
at a chosen prime above
p>2
, and denote by
\mathcal{O}
the ring of integers in
F
. Let
\mathfrak{p}
be the maximal ideal of
\mathcal{O}
and let
\pi
be a generator of
\mathfrak{p}
. Assume that
\mathfrak{p}
is an
Eisenstein prime
for
f
, i.e., that the mod-
\mathfrak{p}
residual Galois representation
\overline{\rho}_{f}
of
G_{\mathbf{Q}}
associated with
f
is reducible. This means that its semisimplification is the direct sum of two characters. After replacing
\overline{\rho}_{f}
by its self-dual twist
\overline{\rho}_{f}(1-r)
, these characters are
\varphi,\psi=\varphi^{-1}\omega
. (In the case
k=2
,
\overline{\rho}_{f}
is the Galois representation of
G_{\mathbf{Q}}
acting on
A_{f}[\mathfrak{p}](\overline{\mathbf{Q}})
where
A_{f}/\mathbf{Q}
is the modular abelian variety attached to
f
). Note that by Serre’s Modularity Conjecture, a theorem of Khare–Kisin–Wintenberger, simple RM abelian varieties over
\mathbf{Q}
are attached to newforms of weight
2
, and vice versa. For elliptic curves, good Eisenstein primes are known to be ordinary.

Let
K/\mathbf{Q}
be a Heegner field for
N
, i.e., an imaginary quadratic field such that all primes dividing
N
split completely in
K
. We further assume that
D_{K}
is odd and
\neq-3
, and that
p=v\overline{v}
splits in
K
.

In
[
CGLS22
]
, it is proved for
k=2
and
\mathbf{Z}[f]=\mathbf{Z}
that, under the assumption
\varphi|_{G_{v}}\neq\mathbf{1},\omega
(where
\mathbf{1}
is the trivial and
\omega
the mod-
p
cyclotomic character), the anticyclotomic Main Conjecture and Perrin-Riou’s Heegner point Main Conjecture hold. From this, the
p
-converse to Gross–Zagier–Kolyvagin’s theorem and the
p
-part of BSD in the analytic rank
1
case (under the assumption that
\varphi
is ramified at
p
and odd or unramified at
p
and even, which was later removed by
[
CGS23
]
) easily follow.

However, this excludes in particular the case where
A_{f}(\mathbf{Q})[\mathfrak{p}]\neq 0
in the case
k=2
. In this article, we close this gap and generalize the results to all weights
2r
with
r
being odd and all coefficient rings.

Our main results are the Iwasawa Main Conjectures
A
and
B
, a
p
-converse theorem
E
to the theorem of Gross–Zagier–Kolyvagin–Logachëv, and the
p
-part of the BSD formula
C
in our situation when
p
does not divide the level of
f
. For newforms of weight
2
, we obtain Iwasawa Main Conjectures at Eisenstein primes of
semistable
reduction (i.e., good reduction or bad multiplicative reduction). See
D
.

0.2.
Method of proof and outline of the paper.

First, we assume
p
does not divide the level of
f
from
section

1
through
section

4
. Following the strategies in
[
CGLS22
]
, the proof of our Iwasawa Main Conjectures for
f
is divided into two steps. We first compare the Iwasawa
\lambda
- and
\mu
-invariants of the algebraic side of and analytic side, then prove a one side divisibility. The starting point is again the observation that a Main Conjecture should be equivalent to an imprimitive one. More specifically, for an appropriate Selmer group
\mathfrak{X}_{f}
for
f
and a corresponding Bertolini–Darmon–Prasanna
p
-adic
L
-function
\mathcal{L}_{f}
, there is an equality for their ‘
S
-imprimitive’ versions

{\operatorname{Char}}(\mathfrak{X}^{S}_{f})\Lambda^{{\operatorname{nr}}}=(\mathcal{L}^{S}_{f})

as ideals in
\Lambda^{{\operatorname{nr}}}
, where the
p
-adic
L
-function for
f
lives.
Here we consider the primitive and imprimitive unramified Selmer groups (which are identified with the corresponding Greenberg Selmer groups in
[
CGLS22
]
in their setting) of the characters
\varphi
and
\psi
and of
f
over the anticyclotomic Iwasawa algebra
\Lambda\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{O}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
, where
\Gamma
is the Galois group of the anticyclotomic
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension
K_{\infty}
of
K
. Here
\Lambda^{{\operatorname{nr}}}\coloneq\Lambda\hat{\otimes}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{{\operatorname{nr}}}
, for
\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{{\operatorname{nr}}}
the completion of the ring of integers of the maximal unramified extension of
\mathbf{Q}_{p}
. Denote by
\mathbf{F}
the residue field of
F
.

In Section
1
, we show that the primitive unramified Selmer group of
f
and the characters
\varphi,\psi
appearing in the semisimplification of
\overline{\rho}_{f}
are
\Lambda
-torsion with
\mu
-invariant
0
. In most cases, we get a simple relation between the
\lambda
-invariants

(0.1)

\lambda(\mathfrak{X}^{S}_{f})=\lambda(\mathfrak{X}^{S}_{\varphi})+\lambda(\mathfrak{X}^{S}_{\psi})

as in
[
CGLS22
]
, but there is a special case when one of the characters is trivial over
G_{K}
, where we get

(0.2)

\lambda(\mathfrak{X}^{S}_{f})+1=\lambda(\mathfrak{X}^{S}_{\varphi})+\lambda(\mathfrak{X}^{S}_{\psi}).

From here, we can easily get the same comparisons between the corresponding primitive Selmer groups.
We mention that this difference in
\lambda
-invariants agrees with the Iwasawa Main Conjecture for the trivial character, which takes on a different form from others

{\operatorname{Char}}(\mathfrak{X}_{\mathbf{1}})=(\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{1}}\cdot T).

Since we allow one of the characters to be trivial on
G_{p}
, the proofs of (
0.1
) and (
0.2
) requires more general ideas than those in
op. cit.
in that the Selmer groups
\mathfrak{X}^{S}_{?}
(
?=f,\vartheta
) may not be
almost divisible
in the sense that they might contain non-trivial finite
\Lambda
-submodules. Indeed, the vanishing results of some
\text{{H}}^{0}
and
\text{{H}}^{2}
cohomology groups in
op. cit.
heavily rely on this condition and no longer hold in our situation. We will compare these groups for
f
to those of the characters directly and obtain an expected relation between their Selmer groups.

By Ribet’s Lemma (see
[
Bel09
]
), we can find a sub-lattice of the lattice for the
\mathfrak{p}
-adic Galois representation of
f
such that the associated mod-
\mathfrak{p}
Galois representation of
f
has no nonzero trivial subrepresentation over
G_{K}
. More precisely, we can fix a non-split extension

0\to\mathbf{F}(\varphi)\to\overline{\rho}_{f}\to\mathbf{F}(\psi)\to 0

where the first character restricts to
\omega
on
G_{p}
so in particular it’s non-trivial over
G_{K}
. We can assume this since both the Iwasawa Main Conjectures and the BSD Conjecture is invariant under isogenies. This choice of the lattice is crucial for our computations.

In Section
2
, we show an analogous formula for the
\mu
- and
\lambda
-invariants for the Katz
p
-adic
L
-functions of the characters and the BDP
L
-function of
f/K
. That is,

\lambda(\mathcal{L}^{S}_{f})=\lambda(\mathcal{L}^{S}_{\varphi})+\lambda(\mathcal{L}^{S}_{\psi}),

where
\mathcal{L}^{S}_{?}\coloneq\mathcal{L}_{?}\cdot\prod_{w\in S}\mathcal{P}_{w}(?)
for certain elements
\mathcal{P}_{w}(?)
in
\Lambda
(see
lemma

1.1.1
,
section

1.5
). Here
?=f,\ \varphi\text{ or }\psi
. Using the known results on the Iwasawa Main Conjecture for the characters proved by Rubin
[
Rub91
]
, we get the equality between algebraic and analytic Iwasawa invariants for
f
.

Hence it suffices to show that the characteristic ideal of the unramified Selmer group divides the
p
-adic
L
-function. For this, we can refer to
[
CGLS22
, §§ 3, 4]
because we chose a lattice
T_{f}
such that
\overline{\rho}_{f}
has no nonzero trivial subrepresentation. However, some of their arguments only apply to a canonical Galois stable lattice
\tilde{T}
of
\rho_{f}
, which is generally different from our choice and might have a trivial subrepresentation. We will take care of this by fully utilizing the flexibility of Kolyvagin systems in
theorem

3.0.6
. Our modifed Kolyvagin system argument yields one-side divisibilities of the Main Conjectures, and using the comparison of Iwasawa invariants from
section

1
and
section

2
we can turn the divisibilities into equalities. The Main Conjectures can then be applied to yield the main results of this paper for the good reduction part.

The first anticyclotomic Iwasawa Main Conjecture we obtain is the following, which can be seen as a special case of Greenberg’s Main Conjectures.

Theorem A

(
theorem

3.0.8
(IMC2))
.

Let
f\in S_{k}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be a newform of weight
k=2r
where
r
is odd and
p>2
an ordinary Eisenstein prime not dividing
N
, and let
K
be an imaginary quadratic field satisfying the hypotheses in
section

0.1
. Let
\mathfrak{X}_{f}
be the Pontryagin dual of the unramified Selmer groups for
f
(see
section

1.4
for precise definitions) and let
\mathcal{L}_{f}
be the corresponding BDP
p
-adic
L
-function. Then
\mathfrak{X}_{f}
is
\Lambda
-torsion, and

{\operatorname{Char}}(\mathfrak{X}_{f})\Lambda^{{\operatorname{nr}}}=(\mathcal{L}_{f})

as ideals in
\Lambda^{{\operatorname{nr}}}
.

The assumption that
r
is odd is a technical assumption that is only used in the proof of
theorem

3.0.6
to turn the Heegner cycles into a Kolyvagin system. It can be removed in certain situations (see
remark

3.0.7
) and is not considered essential. In particular, we still have access to a infinite subfamily of some Hida family for which the Main Conjectures are known, which is the key to showing a Main Conjecture for a weight
2
form of multiplicative reduction (see
section

5.1
).

A second Iwasawa Main Conjecture of Perrin-Riou type, which is equivalent to the one above, is also obtained.

Theorem B

(
theorem

3.0.8
(IMC1))
.

Let
f\in S_{k}(\Gamma_{0}(N))
be a newform of weight
k=2r
where
r
is odd and
p>2
an ordinary Eisenstein prime not dividing
N
, and let
K
be an imaginary quadratic field satisfying the hypotheses in
section

0.1
. Then both
\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda}}(K,\mathbf{T})
and
\mathcal{X}=\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda}}(K,M_{f})^{\vee}
have
\Lambda
-rank one (see
[
CGLS22
, section 3]
for general definitions), and the equality

{\operatorname{Char}}_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{X}_{{\operatorname{tors}}})={\operatorname{Char}}_{\Lambda}(\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\Lambda}}(K,\mathbf{T})/\Lambda\kappa_{\infty})^{2}

holds in
\Lambda
.

We now formulate our main results on the
p
-part of strong BSD for elliptic curves of good reduction at Eisenstein primes.

Theorem C

(
theorem

4.2.1
)
.

Let
E/\mathbf{Q}
be an elliptic curve and
p>2
a prime of good reduction. Assume that
E
admits a cyclic
p
-isogeny with kernel
C=\mathbf{F}_{p}(\varphi)
for some character
\varphi\colon G_{\mathbf{Q}}\to\mathbf{F}_{p}^{\times}
(equivalently,
E[p]
is reducible). Assume that
r_{\operatorname{an}}(E)\in\{0,1\}
.

Then the
p
-part of the BSD formula holds for
E/\mathbf{Q}
, i.e.,

{\operatorname{ord}}_{p}\Big(\frac{L^{*}(E/\mathbf{Q},1)}{\Omega_{E}\cdot{\operatorname{Reg}}_{E/\mathbf{Q}}}\Big)={\operatorname{ord}}_{p}\Big(\frac{{\operatorname{Tam}}(E/\mathbf{Q})\#\Sha(E/\mathbf{Q})[p^{\infty}]}{(\#E(\mathbf{Q})_{\operatorname{tors}})^{2}}\Big).

Here,
L^{*}(E/\mathbf{Q},1)
denotes the leading Taylor coefficient of
L(E/\mathbf{Q},s)
at
s=1
.

Finally, we modify Skinner’s use of Hida families in
[
Ski14
]
to prove a Main Conjecture in the multiplicative reduction case for weight
2
forms in
section

5
. The obstruction is again that our imprimitive Selmer groups
\mathfrak{X}^{S}_{?}
may have finite
\Lambda
-submodules. In particular, the characteristic ideals are no longer identified with the Fitting ideals. The formal argument can be carried out by replacing the Selmer groups by their free parts. We would also need to modify several pieces that go into the proof into the residually reducible setting.

Theorem D

(
theorem

5.1.3
)
.

Let
f
be a newform of weight
2
and
p\|N
an odd Eisenstein prime of multiplicative reduction. Let
K
be an imaginary quadratic field satisfying the hypotheses in
section

0.1
. Then the following equation holds in
\Lambda^{{\operatorname{nr}}}
:

{\operatorname{Char}}(\mathfrak{X}_{f})\Lambda^{{\operatorname{nr}}}=(\mathcal{L}_{f}).

We remark that our Main Conjecture would yield both rank
0
and rank
1
BSD formulae at multiplicative primes provided the results in
[
CGS23
]
are extended to higher weight modular forms.

0.3.
Examples

Here are the elliptic curves
E
over
\mathbf{Q}
in the
[
LMFDB
]
with
E(\mathbf{Q})_{\operatorname{tors}}\cong\mathbf{Z}/p
, rank
0
or
1
, and
p
a prime of good reduction:
p=3
,
p=5
, and
p=7
.

Here are concrete examples for each of these three infinite families, an example of smallest conductor in each case. Our main result implies the
p
-part of strong BSD for all their twists that have analytic rank
0
or
1
, of which there exist infinitely many.
Case
p=3
:
19a3
has torsion subgroup
\mathbf{Z}/3
and rank
1
.
Case
p=5
:
11a3
(
=X_{1}(11)
) has torsion subgroup
\mathbf{Z}/5
and rank
0
.
Case
p=7
:
26b2
has torsion subgroup
\mathbf{Z}/7
and rank
1
.

There are also examples of higher dimension: The descent computations for absolutely simple modular abelian surfaces in
[
KS22
]
and
[
KS23
, § 6]
are not necessary anymore for odd primes of good reduction. For example, the genus
2
curve
X\colon y^{2}=5x^{6}+10x^{5}-13x^{4}-30x^{3}+9x^{2}+20x-8
has absolutely simple semistable RM Jacobian
J
of squarefree level
5\cdot 13
and rank
0
associated to the newform
65.2.a.c
. Note that this curves is not covered by
op. cit.
The algorithms from
op. cit.
prove the prime-to-
3
part of strong BSD for
J/\mathbf{Q}
with
\#\Sha(J/\mathbf{Q})_{\operatorname{an}}=2
. Our results allow us to also prove the
3
-part:
Let the good ordinary prime
3
split as
\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}
in
\mathbf{Z}[f]=\mathbf{Z}[\sqrt{3}]
such that
\rho_{\mathfrak{p}}
is irreducible and
\rho_{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}
is reducible with a trivial
1
-dimensional subrepresentation coming from the
3
-torsion. The results and algorithms of
op. cit.
prove that the Heegner index
I_{K}
for
D_{K}=-131
(so
3
is split in
\mathcal{O}_{K}
) equals
\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}
times some ideal lying above
2
, and that the Tamagawa product considered as an ideal in
\mathbf{Z}[f]
equals
\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}
. Hence
[
KS23
, Theorem 5.2.2]
proves that
\Sha(J/\mathbf{Q})[\mathfrak{p}]=0
. Our main theorem implies
[
CGLS22
, eq. (5.5)]
with
p
-torsion replaced by
\mathfrak{p}
-torsion throughout, showing that
v_{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}(\#\Sha(J/K)[\mathfrak{p}^{\prime\infty}])=2v_{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}(I_{K})-2v_{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}({\operatorname{Tam}}(J/\mathbf{Q}))=2-2=0
. Since
\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}\nmid 2
, we have
\Sha(J/K)[\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}]\simeq\Sha(J/\mathbf{Q})[\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}]\oplus\Sha(J^{K}/\mathbf{Q})[\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}]
, hence we get
\Sha(J/\mathbf{Q})[\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}]=0
, so strong BSD holds for
J/\mathbf{Q}
with
\Sha(J/\mathbf{Q})\cong\mathbf{Z}/2
. (Note that the argument with
[
CGLS22
, eq. (5.5)]
also provides evidence for the conjecture that the Tamagawa product divides the Heegner index.)

0.4.
A
p
-converse theorem and applications to Goldfeld’s conjecture

As in the good ordinary case, we show the equivalence between
D
and a Heegner Point Main Conjecture (
theorem

5.2.1
). As a consequence, we obtain the following.

Theorem E

(
theorem

4.1.1
,
corollary

5.2.2
)
.

Let
A/\mathbf{Q}
be a simple RM abelian variety associated to a newform
f
and
\mathfrak{P}\mid p>2
a prime ideal of its endomorphism ring
\mathcal{O}
of good ordinary or bad multiplicative reduction such that
\rho_{A,\mathfrak{P}}
is reducible.

Then

{\operatorname{corank}}_{\mathcal{O}}{\operatorname{Sel}}_{\mathfrak{P}^{\infty}}(A/\mathbf{Q})=r\in\{0,1\}\implies{\operatorname{rk}}_{\mathcal{O}}A(\mathbf{Q})=r_{\operatorname{an}}(f)=r,

and
\Sha(A/\mathbf{Q})[\mathfrak{P}^{\infty}]
is finite.

Our
3
-converse theorem has several applications to Goldfeld’s conjecture, improving the bounds for the proportion of rank
1
twists when being combined with results
[
BKOS19
,
Shn21
,
BFS23
]
on the average
3
-Selmer rank. See
remark

4.1.2
.

0.5.
Ordering of the characters.

Recall the short exact sequence relating the residual representation
\overline{\rho}_{f}
to the characters

0\to\mathbf{F}(\varphi)\to\overline{\rho}_{f}\to\mathbf{F}(\psi)\to 0,

where we assumed that the first character
\varphi
restricts to
\omega
on
G_{p}
and that the sequence is non-split. From the invariance of the Main Conjectures and the BSD formulae, we could as well fix another ordering where the first character restricts to
\mathbf{1}
on
G_{p}
. As is already noted in
theorem

3.0.6
, the use Ribet’s lemma is somewhat limited, and some results for a different ordering should also be necessary. In fact, our original approach assumed that locally, the trivial character is a subrepresentation, regardless of whether the sequence is split or not. This made our computations in Section
1
easier. However, we had to heavily extend the results on the Kolyvagin system from
[
CGLS22
, § 3]
, or rather
[
How04
]
, to allow non-trivial torsion group
\text{{H}}^{0}(K,\overline{\rho}_{f})
. As our results might be useful for the future, we included them in the Appendix
A
. We also deduce a version of the anticyclotomic control theorem which allows non-trivial torsion in Appendix
B
. It should be mentioned that a direct proof of everything with torsion is possible, but we do not include it in this paper for simplicity.

0.6.
Relation to previous works.

Results in this paper in the good reduction case can be seen as direct generalizations of those in
[
CGLS22
]
to higher weight modular forms while removing the technical condition
\vartheta|_{G_{p}}\neq\mathbf{1},\omega
. Thus it would automatically generalize the results which require the main theorem in
op. cit.
as an input, for example
[
CGS23
, Theorem A]
. We also obtain generalizations of the structure theorems in
[
How04
]
as well as the anticyclotomic control theorem in
[
JSW17
]
in the residually reducible case which allow torsion. Since our characters are more general than those in
[
CGLS22
]
, the results on the proportion of ranks of quadratic twists of an elliptic curve over
\mathbf{Q}
with a rational
3
-isogeny are also improved, following
[
BKOS19
]
.

Finally, we obtain new results for newforms with multiplicative reduction at Eisenstein primes which include a proof of an Iwasawa Main Conjecture. These results are analogous to those in
[
Ski14
]
, but the residually reducible case were untouched before.

0.7.
Future work

As is mentioned before, a generalization of the results in
[
CGS23
]
to higher weight modular forms would yield BSD formulas for elliptic curves at multiplicative reduction. This is what we will examine in later work.

In our subsequent paper
[
KY24
]
, we have obtained the anticyclotomic Main Conjectures for elliptic curves at Eisenstein primes of potentially good ordinary reduction. As a consequence, we obtain the
p
-converse theorems in that setting and the applications to Goldfeld’s Conjecture (see
remark

4.1.2
) are much improved in some situations. We would like to study potentially multiplicative case and potentially supersingular case in future work, as both see very interesting applications in arithmetic statistics.

0.8.
Notation.

Pontryagin duals are denoted by
(-)^{\vee}
. The mod-
p
cyclotomic character is denoted by
\omega
and the
p
-adic cyclotomic character by
\chi
. For any subextension
L/K
of
\bar{K}/K
, denote by
G_{L}
the absolute Galois group of
L
.

0.9.
Acknowledgements.

We thank Debanjana Kundu and Otmar Venjakob for helpful discussions and Ari Shnidman for pointing out the application of our
p
-converse theorem to Goldfeld’s conjecture. We also thank MY’s advisor Francesc Castella for his guidance throughout this project. This work is part of MY’s forthcoming Ph.D. thesis.

1.
Algebraic side

Let
f\in S_{k}(\Gamma_{0}(N))^{\operatorname{new}}
be a newform of weight
k=2r\geq 2
and level
N
with trivial nebentypus. Fix an odd prime
p\nmid N
. Let
\mathbf{Z}[f]
be the coefficient ring of
f
with field of fractions
\mathbf{Q}(f)
. Let
F
be a finite extension of a completion of
\mathbf{Q}(f)
at a chosen prime above
p
with ring of integers
\mathcal{O}
. Let
\mathfrak{p}=(\pi)
be the maximal ideal of
\mathcal{O}
and let
\mathbf{F}/\mathfrak{p}
be the residue field of
\mathcal{O}
.
We use the Pontryagin dual
(-)^{\vee}={\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{O},{\operatorname{cts}}}(-,F/\mathcal{O})
for locally compact
\mathcal{O}
-modules.

Recall that
\omega
is the mod-
p
cyclotomic character.
Denote by

\rho_{f}\colon{\operatorname{Gal}}(\overline{\mathbf{Q}}/\mathbf{Q})\to{\operatorname{Aut}}_{F}(V_{f}(1-r))\cong{\operatorname{GL}}_{2}(F)

the
self-dual Tate twist
of the
p
-adic Galois representation attached to
f
.
It has determinant
\chi
.

Let
K\subset\overline{\mathbf{Q}}
be an imaginary quadratic field in which
p=v\overline{v}
splits, with
v
the prime of
K
above
p
induced by
\iota_{p}
. We also fix an embedding
\iota_{\infty}\colon\overline{\mathbf{Q}}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{C}
.

We assume that
K
satisfies the following
Heegner hypothesis
:

\text{every prime }\ell\mid N\text{ splits in }K

Let
G_{K}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{\operatorname{Gal}}(\overline{\mathbf{Q}}/K)\subset G_{\mathbf{Q}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{\operatorname{Gal}}(\overline{\mathbf{Q}}/\mathbf{Q})
be the absolute Galois group of
K
, and for each place
w
of
K
let
I_{w}\subset G_{w}\subset G_{K}
be the corresponding inertia and decomposition groups, unique up to conjugation. Let
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{w}\in G_{w}/I_{w}
be the arithmetic Frobenius. For the prime
v\mid p
, we assume
G_{v}
is chosen so that it is identified with
{\operatorname{Gal}}(\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}/\mathbf{Q}_{p})
via
\iota_{p}
.

Let
\Gamma\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{\operatorname{Gal}}(K_{\infty}/K)
be the Galois group of the anticyclotomic
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension
K_{\infty}
of
K
, and let
\Lambda\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{O}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
be the anticyclotomic Iwasawa algebra over
\mathcal{O}
. We shall often identify
\Lambda
with the power series ring
\mathcal{O}\llbracket T\rrbracket
by setting
T=\gamma-1
for a fixed topological generator
\gamma\in\Gamma
.

We record the following well-known properties of the anticyclotomic
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension
K_{\infty}/K
of an imaginary quadratic field
K
, i.e., the
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension on which complex conjugation acts as inversion. Readers who are only interested in the main results of this paper can directly go to
section

1.1
.

Lemma 1.0.1

(splitting of primes in
K_{\infty}
)
.

Let
w\mid\ell\neq p
be a place of
K
. If
\ell
is unramified and split in
K
, there are only finitely many primes of
K_{\infty}
lying above
\ell
. If
\ell
is ramified or inert in
K
,
\ell
splits completely in
K_{\infty}
.

Note that this is in contrast to the
cyclotomic

\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension, in which no primes are infinitely split.

Lemma 1.0.2

(local behavior of
K_{\infty}
above
p
)
.

Let
K
be an imaginary quadratic field and
p=v\overline{v}
a rational prime completely split in
K
. Let
K_{\infty}/K
be the anticyclotomic
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension.

(i)

\Gamma
is the
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-quotient of
\varprojlim_{n}{\operatorname{Gal}}(K_{n}/K)
, where
K_{n}
is the ring class field of the order
\mathcal{O}_{p^{n}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathbf{Z}+p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{K}
. The unramified part of
\Gamma
at
v\mid p
is cyclic of order dividing
h_{K}=\#{\operatorname{Gal}}(K_{0}/K)
, and
K_{\infty,v}/K_{1,v}
is totally ramified at
v
.

(ii)

The completion
K_{\infty,v}
of
K_{\infty}
at
v\mid p
is a ramified
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension of
K_{v}=\mathbf{Q}_{p}
different from
K_{v,cyc}
, the cyclotomic
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension of
K_{v}
. In particular,
K_{\infty,v}\cap K_{v,cyc}
is a finite extension of
K_{v}
.

(iii)

Let
v\mid p
be a place of
K
. The image of the compositions
G_{v}\hookrightarrow G_{K}\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma
and
I_{v}\hookrightarrow G_{v}\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma
are open. If
p\nmid h_{K}
, the composition
I_{v}\hookrightarrow G_{v}\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma
is surjective.

Proof.

(i)

Note that
K_{0}
is the Hilbert class field of
K
, so by class field theory
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{v}\in{\operatorname{Gal}}(K_{0}/K)
has order equal to the order of
v
in the class group of
K
. All primes above
p
are totally ramified in
K_{n}/K_{0}
, and one has canonical isomorphisms
{\operatorname{Gal}}(K_{n}/K)\simeq{\operatorname{Pic}}(\mathcal{O}_{n})\simeq(\mathcal{O}_{K}/p^{n})^{\times}/(\mathbf{Z}/p^{n})^{\times}
from
[
Cox13
, equation (7.27)]
.

(ii)

The first claim follows from
[
LNQD00
, Theorem 3.2]
; condition (ii) there is satisfied for
K
. Thus

{\operatorname{Gal}}((K_{\infty,v}\cap K_{v,{\operatorname{cyc}}})/K_{v})\cong{\operatorname{Gal}}(K_{\infty,v}/K_{v})/{\operatorname{Gal}}(K_{\infty,v}/(K_{\infty,v}\cap K_{v,{\operatorname{cyc}}}))

must be a non-trivial quotient of
{\operatorname{Gal}}(K_{\infty,v}/K_{v})\cong\mathbf{Z}_{p}
by an open subgroup, and hence it must be finite.

(iii)

By the (ii),
G_{v}\hookrightarrow G_{K_{v}}
is open. The remaining statements follow from the (i).∎

Corollary 1.0.3

(the localization is an isomorphism away from
p
)
.

Let
A
be a
p^{\infty}
-torsion
G_{K_{w}}
-module. If
\ell\neq p
is unramified and split with
w\mid\ell
in
K
, then the restriction morphism
\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{\infty,w}/K_{w},A)\to\text{{H}}^{1}(I_{w},A)^{\Gamma/I_{w}}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

See
[
PW11
, Remark 3.1 and Lemma 3.2]
. Note that the kernel and cokernel of the restriction morphism are the first and second Galois cohomology groups of a
p
-primary Galois module and that the profinite degree of the absolute Galois group
G_{\infty,w}
of
K_{\infty,w}
is prime to
p
.
∎

Lemma 1.0.4

(the dual of the Iwasawa algebra)
.

The Pontryagin dual
\Lambda^{\vee}
of the Iwasawa algebra is isomorphic to
(F/\mathcal{O})[\varinjlim_{n}(\Gamma/\Gamma_{n})^{\vee}]\simeq(\varinjlim_{n}F/\mathcal{O})[(\Gamma/\Gamma_{n})^{\vee}]
.

Proof.

This follows from the exactness property of the Pontryagin dual, which interchanges projective and injective limits, and the definition of the Iwasawa algebra as a projective limit.
∎

Corollary 1.0.5

(Galois cohomology of Iwasawa algebra equals Galois cohomology over the
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension)
.

One has
\text{{H}}^{i}(K,\Lambda^{\vee})\simeq\text{{H}}^{i}(K_{\infty},F/\mathcal{O})
. For the twist of
\Lambda^{\vee}
by a character
\vartheta\colon G_{K}\to\mathbf{F}^{\times}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}^{\times}
, one has
\text{{H}}^{i}(K,\mathcal{O}(\vartheta)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\Lambda^{\vee})\simeq\text{{H}}^{i}(K_{\infty},F/\mathcal{O}(\vartheta))
. If
K_{\vartheta}
is obtained from
K
by adjoining the values of
\vartheta
, then
K_{\vartheta}
is linearly disjoint from
K_{\infty}
and one has
\text{{H}}^{i}(K_{\infty},F/\mathcal{O}(\vartheta))\simeq\text{{H}}^{i}(K_{\infty}K_{\vartheta},F/\mathcal{O})^{\Delta_{\vartheta}}
.

Proof.

See
[
CGLS22
, proof of Theorem 1.2.2]
. Note that their proof works for any
\vartheta
.
∎

1.1.
Local cohomology groups of characters.

Recall that
\mathbf{F}=\mathcal{O}/\mathfrak{p}
is the residue field of
\mathcal{O}
, a finite field of characteristic
p
. Let
\vartheta\colon G_{K}\to\mathbf{F}^{\times}
be a character with conductor divisible only by primes that are split in
K
. Since the case where
\vartheta|_{G_{p}}\neq\mathbf{1},\omega
is already dealt with in
[
CGLS22
]
, we focus on the complementary cases. Via the Teichmüller lift
\mathbf{F}^{\times}\hookrightarrow F^{\times}
, we shall also view
\vartheta
as taking values in
\mathcal{O}^{\times}
. Set

M_{\vartheta}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{O}(\vartheta)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\Lambda^{\vee}.

The module
M_{\vartheta}
is equipped with a
G_{K}
-action via
\vartheta\otimes\Psi^{-1}
, where
\Psi\colon G_{K}\to\Lambda^{\times}
is the character arising from the projection
G_{K}\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma
. One has a
G_{K}
-equivariant isomorphism
M_{\vartheta}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{O},{\operatorname{cts}}}(\Lambda,\mathcal{O}(\vartheta))
.

Note that one has an exact sequence

(1.1)

0\to(F/\mathcal{O})(\vartheta)\to M_{\vartheta}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot T}}{{\to}}M_{\vartheta}\to 0.

In this section, we study the local cohomology of
M_{\vartheta}
at various primes
w
of
K
.

1.1.1.
w\nmid p
split in K

Lemma 1.1.1
.

Let
\gamma_{w}
be the image of
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{w}
in the decomposition group of
w\mid\ell
. Let
\mathcal{P}_{w}(\vartheta)=P_{w}(\ell^{-1}\gamma_{w})\in\Lambda
with
P_{w}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\det(1-{\operatorname{Frob}}_{w}X\mid F(\vartheta)_{I_{w}})
the Euler factor at
w
of the
L
-function of
\vartheta
.

The module
\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},M_{\vartheta})^{\vee}
is
\Lambda
-torsion with characteristic ideal
(\mathcal{P}_{w}(\vartheta))
. In particular, it has
\mu
-invariant
0
.

Proof.

See
[
CGLS22
, Lemma 1.1.1]
. Note that their proof works for any
\vartheta
.
∎

1.1.2.
w\mid p
in
K

Lemma 1.1.2
.

Let
X
be a finitely generated
\Lambda
-module.

(i)

If

•

X[T]=0
and

•

X/TX
is a free
\mathcal{O}
-module of rank
r
,

then
X
is a free
\Lambda
-module of rank
r
(and conversely).

(ii)

If

•

X[T]=0
and

•

{\operatorname{rk}}_{\mathcal{O}}X/TX=r
,

then
{\operatorname{rk}}_{\Lambda}X=r
.

Proof.

(i)

This is
[
CGLS22
, Lemma 1.1.2]
(see also
[
NSW08
, Proposition 5.3.19 (ii)]
).

(ii)

Consider the structure theorem

X\sim\Lambda^{s}\oplus\bigoplus_{i}\Lambda/(f_{i})\oplus\bigoplus_{j}\Lambda/(\pi^{m_{j}})

for some distinguished polynomials
f_{i}
.
That
X[T]=0
implies there is no
f_{i}
. Then
X/TX
would have
\mathcal{O}
-rank
s
because
(\Lambda/(\pi^{m_{j}}))/T
is finite for any
j
. Hence
r=s
.
∎

We now study the case
\vartheta|_{G_{v}}\in\{\mathbf{1},\omega\}
excluded in
[
CGLS22
]
. Note that in each of these cases one property in
[
CGLS22
]
fails.

Proposition 1.1.3
.

Assume that
\vartheta|_{G_{v}}\in\{\mathbf{1},\omega\}
(all other cases have been considered in the analogous
[
CGLS22
, Proposition 1.1.3]
). Then:

(i)

The restriction morphism

r_{w}\colon\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},M_{\vartheta})\to\text{{H}}^{1}(I_{w},M_{\vartheta})^{G_{w}/I_{w}}

is surjective.

•

If
\vartheta|_{G_{w}}=\mathbf{1}
, its kernel is a cofree
\mathcal{O}
-module of rank
1
. In particular,
ker(r_{w})^{\vee}
has
\Lambda
-rank
0
and it has no non-trivial finite
\Lambda
-submodule because all non-trivial subgroups of
\mathcal{O}
are infinite.

•

If
\vartheta|_{G_{w}}=\omega
, it is an isomorphism.

(ii)

•

If
\vartheta|_{G_{w}}=\mathbf{1}
,
\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},M_{\vartheta})
is
\Lambda
-cofree of rank
1
.

•

If
\vartheta|_{G_{w}}=\omega
,
X\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},M_{\vartheta})^{\vee}
satisfies
{\operatorname{pd}}_{\Lambda}X\leq 1
, (hence) has no non-trivial finite (
\Lambda
-)submodule, and is generated by
2
elements with
\Lambda
-rank equal to
1
.

Proof.

Note that
K_{w}=\mathbf{Q}_{p}
and that
K_{w,\infty}/K_{w}
is an infinitely ramified
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension by
lemma

1.0.2
not containing
\mu_{p}
because
p>2
and
([K_{w}(\mu_{p}):K_{w}],p)=1
.

(i): Since
{\operatorname{cd}}(K_{w})=1
and
G_{w}/I_{w}\simeq\hat{\mathbf{Z}}\cdot{\operatorname{Frob}}_{w}
,
r_{w}
is surjective and
\ker(r_{w})\simeq M_{\vartheta}^{I_{w}}/({\operatorname{Frob}}_{w}-1)M_{\vartheta}^{I_{w}}
by
[
Rub00
, Lemma I.3.2 (i)]
.
Let us now determine the
\ker(r_{w})
by determining its Pontryagin dual as a
\Lambda
-module. Dualizing

0\to M_{\vartheta}^{G_{w}}\to M_{\vartheta}^{I_{w}}\xrightarrow{{\operatorname{Frob}}_{w}-1}M_{\vartheta}^{I_{w}}\to M_{\vartheta}^{I_{w}}/({\operatorname{Frob}}_{w}-1)M_{\vartheta}^{I_{w}}\to 0

and noting that Pontryagin duality transforms
G_{w}
-invariants into the quotient by
T
,
\ker(r_{w})^{\vee}
sits in an exact sequence

(1.2)

0\to\ker(r_{w})^{\vee}\to(M_{\vartheta}^{\vee})_{I_{w}}\to(M_{\vartheta}^{\vee})_{I_{w}}\to M_{\vartheta}^{\vee}/T\to 0.

If
\vartheta|_{G_{w}}\neq\mathbf{1}
,
M_{\vartheta}^{G_{w}}=0
, so
(M_{\vartheta}^{\vee})_{I_{w}}\to(M_{\vartheta}^{\vee})_{I_{w}}
is a surjection of finitely generated modules over the Noetherian algebra
\Lambda
, hence an isomorphism.

If
\vartheta|_{G_{w}}=\mathbf{1}
, the exact sequence (
1.2
) reads

0\to\ker(r_{w})^{\vee}\to\Lambda_{I_{w}}\to\Lambda_{I_{w}}\to\Lambda/T\to 0,

i.e.,

0\to\ker(r_{w})^{\vee}\to\mathcal{O}[\Gamma/I_{w}]\to\mathcal{O}[\Gamma/I_{w}]\to\mathcal{O}\to 0.

Since
I_{w}\subseteq G_{w}
is of finite index by
lemma

1.0.2
,
\Gamma/I_{w}\cong\mathbf{Z}/p^{m}
with
p^{m}\mid h_{K}
. Hence all modules are
\mathcal{O}
torsion-free and
{\operatorname{rk}}_{\mathcal{O}}\ker(r_{w})^{\vee}=1
.
We now prove (ii). Let
X
be the
\Lambda
-module
\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},M_{\vartheta})^{\vee}
. It is finitely generated because it is finite mod
(p,T)
. We determine
X[T]
and
X/TX
if
\vartheta|_{G_{w}}\in\{\mathbf{1},\omega\}
.

Claim 0:
\text{{H}}^{i}(K_{w},M_{\vartheta})=\text{{H}}^{i}(K_{w,\infty},F/\mathcal{O}(\vartheta))
and
\text{{H}}^{2}(K_{w},M_{\vartheta})=0
independent of
\vartheta
.

The first statement is Shapiro’s lemma. Note that
K_{w,\infty}/K_{w}
is a (infinitely ramified)
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension by
lemma

1.0.2
. For the second statement, use the first and that from
[
Nek06
]

\text{{H}}^{2}(K_{w},M_{\vartheta})=\text{{H}}^{2}(K_{w,\infty},F/\mathcal{O})=\text{{H}}^{0}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(K_{w,\infty}/K_{w},\mathcal{O}(\chi\vartheta^{-1}))=0,

where the
0
-th Iwasawa cohomology is
0
by
[
SV16
, Remark 5.11, Lemma 5.12]
.

Claim 1:
\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},M_{\mathbf{1}})/T=\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},M_{\omega})/T=0
.

From Claim 0, there is an isomorphism

\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},M_{\vartheta})/T\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\text{{H}}^{2}(K_{w},F/\mathcal{O}(\vartheta)).

From (
1.4
), one gets

0\to\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},F/\mathcal{O}(\vartheta))/\mathfrak{p}\to\text{{H}}^{2}(K_{w},\mathbf{F}(\vartheta))\to\text{{H}}^{2}(K_{w},F/\mathcal{O}(\vartheta))[\mathfrak{p}]\to 0

and

0\to\text{{H}}^{2}(K_{w},F/\mathcal{O}(\vartheta))/\mathfrak{p}\to\text{{H}}^{3}(K_{w},\mathbf{F}(\vartheta))\to\text{{H}}^{3}(K_{w},F/\mathcal{O}(\vartheta))[\mathfrak{p}]\to 0.

In the first sequence,
\text{{H}}^{2}(K_{w},\mathbf{F}(\vartheta))\simeq\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{w},\mathbf{F}(\omega\vartheta^{-1}))^{\vee}
by Tate’s local duality. In the second sequence, the
\text{{H}}^{3}
terms are
0
since
{\operatorname{cd}}(K_{w})=2
. Hence
\text{{H}}^{2}(K_{w},F/\mathcal{O}(\vartheta))
is
p
-divisible and finitely generated, hence isomorphic to
(F/\mathcal{O})^{r}
for some
r
. If
\vartheta=\mathbf{1}
,
r=0
, since the middle term in the first sequence is
0
, and so
\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},M_{\mathbf{1}})/T=0
. If
\vartheta=\omega
,
\text{{H}}^{2}(K_{w},F/\mathcal{O}(\omega))\cong\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{w},\mathbf{Z}_{p}(\omega))^{\vee}
by Tate’s local duality (extended to
\mathcal{O}
-modules using that projective limits are dual to injective ones) and the latter is
0
.

Claim 2a:
\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},M_{\mathbf{1}})[T]=F/\mathcal{O}
.

Equivalently,
(X/TX)^{\vee}=0
for the Pontryagin dual. The long exact cohomology sequence over
K_{w}
for (
1.1
) gives an exact sequence

(1.3)

0\to\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{w},M_{\vartheta})/T\to\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},F/\mathcal{O}(\vartheta))\to X^{\vee}[T]\to 0.

By Claim 0,
\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{w},M_{\vartheta})/T=(F/\mathcal{O})/T=F/\mathcal{O}
, hence
(\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{w},M_{\vartheta})/T)^{\vee}=\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{w},M_{\vartheta})^{\vee}[T]=\mathcal{O}
.

If
\vartheta|_{G_{w}}=\mathbf{1}
, the middle term
\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},F/\mathcal{O}(\vartheta))
is canonically isomorphic to
{\operatorname{Gal}}(K_{w}^{{\operatorname{ab}},p}/K_{w})^{\vee}=(K_{w}^{\times})^{\wedge,p}=\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{2}
by local class field theory since
\mu_{p}\not\subset K_{w}^{\times}
. Hence the Pontryagin dual of the exact sequence (
1.3
) reads

0\to X/TX\to\mathcal{O}^{2}\to\mathcal{O}\to 0,

hence
{\operatorname{rk}}_{\mathcal{O}}X/TX=1
and
X/TX
is
\mathcal{O}
-free, hence
X/TX\cong\mathcal{O}
and so
X^{\vee}[T]\cong F/\mathcal{O}
.

Since
X[T]=0
from Claim 1, apply
lemma

1.1.2
to
X\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},M_{\vartheta})^{\vee}
to get (ii) in the case
\vartheta|_{G_{w}}=\mathbf{1}
.

Claim 2b:
\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},M_{\omega})[T]\cong F/\mathcal{O}\oplus\mathcal{O}/\mathfrak{p}^{n}
for some
n>0
.

If
\vartheta|_{G_{w}}=\omega
, we look at the sequence (
1.3
) again:

\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{w},M_{\vartheta})/T=(F/\mathcal{O}(\omega))^{G_{K_{w,\infty}}}/T=0/T=0

because
\mu_{p}\not\subset K_{w,\infty}
:
{\operatorname{Gal}}(K_{w}(\mu_{p})/K_{w})\simeq(\mathbf{Z}/p)^{\times}
cannot be a quotient of the
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension
K_{w,\infty}/K_{w}
if
p>2
by
lemma

1.0.2
(iii). Hence

\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},F/\mathcal{O}(\omega))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}X^{\vee}[T]=(X/TX)^{\vee}.

The long exact Galois cohomology sequence of

(1.4)

0\to\mathbf{F}(\omega)\to F/\mathcal{O}(\omega)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\to}}F/\mathcal{O}(\omega)\to 0

gives the short exact sequence

0\to\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{w},F/\mathcal{O}(\omega))/\mathfrak{p}\to\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},\mathbf{F}(\omega))\to\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},F/\mathcal{O}(\omega))[\mathfrak{p}]\to 0.

We have already seen
\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{w},F/\mathcal{O}(\omega))=0
, hence one has an isomorphism

\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},\mathbf{F}(\omega))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},F/\mathcal{O}(\omega))[\mathfrak{p}].

One can compute the (finite)
\mathbf{F}
-dimension of
\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},\mathbf{F}(\omega))
using the local Euler–Poincaré characteristic formula, or by noting that
\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},\mathbf{F}(\omega))
is dual to
\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},\mathbf{F})
by Tate’s local duality, and the latter is an
\mathbf{F}
-vector space of dimension
{\operatorname{dim}}_{\mathbf{F}}(K_{w}^{\times})/p=2
by local class field theory, noting that
\mu_{p}\not\subset K_{w}^{\times}=\mathbf{Q}_{p}^{\times}
since
p>2
.

The Galois cohomology of (
1.4
) also gives an exact sequence

0\to\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},F/\mathcal{O}(\omega))/\mathfrak{p}\to\text{{H}}^{2}(K_{w},\mathbf{F}(\omega))\to\text{{H}}^{2}(K_{w},F/\mathcal{O}(\omega))[\mathfrak{p}]\to 0.

The last term evaluates by Tate’s local duality theorem to

\text{{H}}^{2}(K_{w},F/\mathcal{O}(\omega))[\mathfrak{p}]\simeq(\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{w},\mathcal{O}(\chi\omega^{-1}))/\mathfrak{p})^{\vee}=0

with
\chi\colon G_{K_{w}}\to\mathcal{O}^{\times}
the
p
-adic cyclotomic character and
\chi\omega^{-1}
its projection
\chi^{1}
to the principal units
1+p\mathbf{Z}_{p}
(note that
p>2
). Hence

(1.5)

\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},F/\mathcal{O}(\omega))/\mathfrak{p}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\text{{H}}^{2}(K_{w},\mathbf{F}(\omega))=\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{w},\mathbf{F})^{\vee}=\mathbf{F}

by Tate’s local duality.

Hence we know
\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},F/\mathcal{O}(\omega))
mod
\mathfrak{p}
(namely
\mathbf{F}
) and its
\mathfrak{p}
-torsion (namely
\mathbf{F}^{2}
). As it is a cofinitely generated
\mathcal{O}
-module (since its
\mathfrak{p}
-torsion is finite dimensional), writing it as
(F/\mathcal{O})^{r}\oplus A
with
A
a finite
\mathcal{O}
-module,
A/\mathfrak{p}\cong\mathbf{F}
from (
1.5
), hence
A\cong\mathcal{O}/\mathfrak{p}^{n}
for some
n>0
, and so
\mathbf{F}^{2}\cong\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},\mathbf{F}(\omega))=\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},F/\mathcal{O}(\omega))[\mathfrak{p}]=\mathbf{F}^{r+1}
. Hence
r=1
and
\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},F/\mathcal{O}(\omega))\cong F/\mathcal{O}\oplus\mathcal{O}/\mathfrak{p}^{n}
with
n>0
, agreeing with the local Euler–Poincaré characteristic formula
0-2+1=\chi(K_{w},\mathbf{F}(\omega))=-[K_{w}:\mathbf{Q}_{p}]=-1
.

Determination of
\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},M_{\omega})
as a
\Lambda
-module.

Hence we know from (
1.3
) that
X/TX\cong\mathcal{O}\oplus\mathcal{O}/\mathfrak{p}^{n}
with
n>0
in the case
\vartheta|_{G_{w}}=\omega
, so by
lemma

1.1.2

X
is not
\Lambda
-free and is generated by
\leq 2
elements over
\Lambda
, and that
X[T]=0
from Claim 1. When
X[T]=0
,
X
is
\Lambda
-torsion free, and there is a presentation

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C/T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{2}/T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{p}^{n}\mathcal{O}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}\oplus\mathcal{O}/\mathfrak{p}^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

of
X
as a
\Lambda
-module. Since
C\subset\Lambda^{2}
is
\Lambda
-torsion free and
C/T\cong\mathfrak{p}^{n}\mathcal{O}
,
C\cong\Lambda
by
lemma

1.1.2
. Hence the presentation implies that
{\operatorname{rk}}_{\Lambda}X=1
and
{\operatorname{pd}}_{\Lambda}X\leq 1
, hence
X
has no finite
\Lambda
-submodule by
[
NSW08
, Proposition (5.3.19) (i)]
.
∎

1.2.
Selmer groups of characters

Let
\vartheta\colon G_{K}\to\mathbf{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}
be a character whose conductor is divisible only by primes split in
K
(i.e., unramified over
\mathbf{Q}
and have degree
1
).

Let
\Sigma
be a finite set of places of
K
containing
\infty
and the primes dividing
p
or the conductor of
\vartheta
and such that every finite place in
\Sigma
is split in
K
. Denote the maximal extension of
K
unramified outside
\Sigma
by
K^{\Sigma}
. Recall that
p
splits as
v\overline{v}
in
K
.

Definition 1.2.1
.

(i)

The
Greenberg Selmer group
of
\vartheta
is

\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{Gr}}}(K,M_{\vartheta})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\ker\Big\{\text{{H}}^{1}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\vartheta})\xrightarrow{{\operatorname{res}}_{\operatorname{Gr}}}\prod_{w\in\Sigma,w\nmid p}\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},M_{\vartheta})\times\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{\overline{v}},M_{\vartheta})\Big\}.

(ii)

For
S\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\Sigma\setminus\{v,\overline{v},\infty\}
, let the
S
-imprimitive Selmer group
of
\vartheta
be

\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{Gr}}^{S}}(K,M_{\vartheta})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\ker\Big\{\text{{H}}^{1}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\vartheta})\to\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{\overline{v}},M_{\vartheta})\Big\},

Replacing
M_{\vartheta}
by
M_{\vartheta}[\mathfrak{p}]
in the above definitions, we obtain the
residual Greenberg Selmer groups

\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{Gr}}}(K,M_{\vartheta}[\mathfrak{p}])
and
\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{Gr}}^{S}}(K,M_{\vartheta}[\mathfrak{p}])
.

(iii)

The
Selmer group with unramified local conditions away from
v
, also called
unramified Selmer group
for short, of
\vartheta
is

\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}}(K,M_{\vartheta})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\ker\Big\{\text{{H}}^{1}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\vartheta})\xrightarrow{{\operatorname{res}}_{{\operatorname{nr}},\vartheta}}\prod_{w\in\Sigma,w\nmid p}\text{{H}}^{1}(I_{w},M_{\vartheta})^{G_{w}/I_{w}}\times\text{{H}}^{1}(I_{\overline{v}},M_{\vartheta})^{G_{\overline{v}}/I_{\overline{v}}}\Big\}.

(iv)

For
S\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\Sigma\setminus\{v,\overline{v},\infty\}
, let the
S
-imprimitive unramified Selmer group
of
\vartheta
be

\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}^{S}}(K,M_{\vartheta})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\ker\Big\{\text{{H}}^{1}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\vartheta})\to\text{{H}}^{1}(I_{\overline{v}},M_{\vartheta})^{G_{\overline{v}}/I_{\overline{v}}}\Big\},

Replacing
M_{\vartheta}
by
M_{\vartheta}[\mathfrak{p}]
in the above definitions, we obtain the
residual unramified Selmer groups

\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}}(K,M_{\vartheta}[\mathfrak{p}])
and
\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}^{S}}(K,M_{\vartheta}[\mathfrak{p}])
.

Note these Selmer groups are
\Lambda
-cofinitely generated (by finiteness of the
\mathfrak{p}
-Selmer groups and Nakayama’s lemma), and the Selmer groups are independent of the choice of
\Sigma
as reflected in their notation.

The local conditions for these
unramified Selmer groups
are relaxed (namely, the full group) at
v
and
\mathcal{L}_{v}=\ker(r_{w})
for
w\in\Sigma\setminus\{v\}
where
r_{w}\colon\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},M_{\vartheta})\to\text{{H}}^{1}(I_{w},M_{\vartheta})^{G_{w}/I_{w}}
is the restriction in
proposition

1.1.3
. Note that
\text{{H}}^{1}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\vartheta})={\operatorname{Sel}}^{\Sigma}(K,M_{\vartheta})
with the
\Sigma
-relaxed Selmer group and
\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}}(K,M_{\vartheta})={\operatorname{Sel}}^{\Sigma}_{\Sigma\setminus\{v\}}(K,M_{\vartheta})
with the
\Sigma\setminus\{v\}
-strict Selmer group by
[
Rub00
, Lemma I.5.3]
. Analogously,
\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}^{S}}(K,M_{\vartheta})={\operatorname{Sel}}^{\Sigma}_{\{\overline{v}\}}(K,M_{\vartheta})
is the
\overline{v}
-strict Selmer group. The
S
-imprimitive Selmer group has less local conditions than the Selmer group, omitting the local conditions at
S
. Hence one has an inclusion
\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}}(K,M_{\vartheta})\subseteq\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}^{S}}(K,M_{\vartheta})
.

Note that because
K_{w}=\mathbf{Q}_{p}
and
[\mathbf{Q}_{p}(\mu_{p}):\mathbf{Q}_{p}]=p-1>1
,
\omega|_{G_{w}}\neq 1
. Hence also
\omega|_{G_{K}}\neq 1
. We need this because we use several times that
\omega
has no invariants over
G_{w}
and
G_{K}
.

Theorem 1.2.2
.

Assume that the conductor of
\vartheta
is only divisible by primes that split in
K
. Then
\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}}(K,M_{\vartheta})^{\vee}
is a finitely generated
\Lambda
-torsion module with
\mu
-invariant
0
.

Proof.

The first part of this proposition is essentially proved in
[
Rub91
]
(with
[
Rub94
]
to remove the assumption on
h_{K}
). As explained in
[
CGLS22
, Theorem 1.2.2]
, the dual Selmer groups
\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}}(K,M_{\vartheta})^{\vee}
are readily identified with the
\vartheta
-isotypic components of
\mathcal{X}_{\infty}
(which are shown to be
\Lambda
-torsion in
[
Rub91
, Remark (ii) after Theorem 4.1]
) where
\mathcal{X}_{\infty}
is the Galois group of the maximal abelian pro-
p
extension of
K_{\infty}K_{\vartheta}
unramified outside
v
,
K_{\vartheta}
being the fixed field of
\ker(\vartheta)
.

That
\mu
-invariants is
0
follows from the vanishing result of
[
Hid10
]
and the Iwasawa Main Conjectures in
[
Rub91
]
together with
[
CW78
]
(or more generally,
[
dS87
, III.1.10]
to remove the assumption on
p\neq 3
, that
p
is an anomalous prime and that
i\neq 0
which corresponds to the case where
\vartheta|_{G_{K}}=\mathbf{1}
). More precisely, if
\vartheta|_{G_{K}}\neq\mathbf{1}
, then the Main Conjecture for
\vartheta
identifies Char
(\mathfrak{X}_{\vartheta})
with
(\mathcal{L}_{\vartheta})
and hence the algebraic
\mu
-invariant equals the analytic one which vanishes. If
\vartheta|_{G_{K}}=\mathbf{1}
, the Main Conjecture identifies Char
(\mathfrak{X}_{\mathbf{1}})
with
(\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{1}}\cdot T)
so again both
\mu
-invariants vanish.
∎

Remark 1.2.3
.

(i)

Recall that from
corollary

1.0.3
, the local conditions of our Selmer group at
w\nmid p
agree with the Greenberg’s local conditions. This is also discussed in
[
CGLS22
, Theorem 1.2.2]
. As a consequence, our Selmer group can be alternatively defined as

\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}}(K,M_{\vartheta})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\ker\Big\{\text{{H}}^{1}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\vartheta})\xrightarrow{{\operatorname{res}}}\prod_{w\in\Sigma,w\nmid p}\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},M_{\vartheta})\times\text{{H}}^{1}(I_{\overline{v}},M_{\vartheta})^{G_{\overline{v}}/I_{\overline{v}}}\Big\}.

(ii)

We remark here that
theorem

1.2.2
is crucial in the proof of the fact that the global-to-local map defining the above Selmer group (as well as the imprimitive one) is surjective.

Proof.

Regarding (ii):
We want to show that the conditions (1)–(4) of
[
PW11
, Proposition A.2]
are satisfied for
\text{{H}}^{1}(K,M_{\vartheta})\simeq\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{\infty},F/\mathcal{O}(\vartheta))
by Shapiro’s lemma(see
corollary

1.0.5
). The conditions are for
K/\mathbf{Q}
an imaginary quadratic number field with
p=v\overline{v}
and local conditions
\mathcal{L}_{w}
:

(1)

No place
w\mid p
splits completely in
K_{\infty}
.

(2)

The Selmer group is
\Lambda
-cotorsion.

(3)

\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{\infty},{\operatorname{Hom}}(\mathcal{O}(\vartheta),F/\mathcal{O}(1)))
is finite.

(4)

r_{v}+r_{\overline{v}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}{\operatorname{corank}}_{\Lambda}\mathcal{L}_{v}+{\operatorname{corank}}_{\Lambda}\mathcal{L}_{\overline{v}}={\operatorname{rk}}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{O}(\vartheta)\mathrel{{=}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}}[K:\mathbf{Q}]d-d\mathrel{{=}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}}\delta(K,V)
.

Condition (1) is satisfied for the anticyclotomic
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension by
lemma

1.0.2
. By
theorem

1.2.2
,
\mathfrak{X}_{\vartheta}
is
\Lambda
-torsion; this is condition (2). Denoting by
\chi
the
p
-adic cyclotomic character. Then the Cartier dual
{\operatorname{Hom}}(\mathcal{O}(\vartheta),F/\mathcal{O}(1))=F/\mathcal{O}(\chi\vartheta^{-1})
has finite
G_{K_{\infty}}
-invariants for any finite order character
\vartheta
. (This is equivalent to showing that
\chi\vartheta^{-1}|_{G_{K_{\infty}}}\neq\mathbf{1}
. Note that
\ker(\chi)=K_{{\operatorname{cyc}}}
, the cyclotomic
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension of
K
, is linearly disjoint from
K_{{\operatorname{cyc}}}
, so
\chi(G_{K_{\infty}})=\chi(G_{K_{\infty}}G_{K_{{\operatorname{cyc}}}})=\chi(G_{K})
. Hence
\chi|_{G_{K_{\infty}}}\colon G_{K_{\infty}}\to\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\times}
has open image. But multiplying it by the character
\vartheta^{-1}
with finite image does not change this property.)
This means that condition (3) is satisfied. Condition (4) is satisfied since one has
r_{v}=1,r_{\overline{v}}=0
(from
proposition

1.1.3
),
[K:\mathbf{Q}]=2
,
d=1
(we have characters) and
\delta(K,F(\vartheta))=1
(there is exactly one archimedean place up to equivalence, and it is complex, and
d=1
).

Surjectivity for the
S
-imprimitive Selmer group immediately follows. ∎

Consider the (finitely generated)
\Lambda
-modules

\displaystyle\mathfrak{X}_{\vartheta}^{S}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}^{S}}(K,M_{\vartheta})^{\vee},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{X}_{\vartheta}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}}(K,M_{\vartheta})^{\vee}.

We now determine
\lambda(\mathfrak{X}_{\vartheta}^{S})
in terms of
\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}^{S}}(K,M_{\vartheta}[\mathfrak{p}])
.

Lemma 1.2.4

(relation between the
p
-torsion of the
S
-imprimitive Selmer group and the residual one)
.

One has an isomorphism

\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}^{S}}(K,M_{\vartheta}[\mathfrak{p}])\simeq\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}^{S}}(K,M_{\vartheta})[\mathfrak{p}].

Note that one has a surjection
{\operatorname{Sel}}^{\Sigma}(K,M_{\vartheta}[\mathfrak{p}])\twoheadrightarrow{\operatorname{Sel}}^{\Sigma}(K,M_{\vartheta})[\mathfrak{p}]
by
[
Rub00
, Lemma I.5.4]
.

Proof.

The long exact cohomology sequence of
0\to M_{\vartheta}[\mathfrak{p}]\to M_{\vartheta}\to M_{\vartheta}\to 0
(note that
M_{\vartheta}
is
\pi
-divisible since
\Lambda^{\vee}
is) yields a short exact sequence

0\to\text{{H}}^{0}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\vartheta})/\pi\to\text{{H}}^{1}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\vartheta}[\mathfrak{p}])\to\text{{H}}^{1}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\vartheta})[\mathfrak{p}]\to 0,

with an analogous short exact sequence with
K^{\Sigma}/K
replaced by
K_{\overline{v}}
. This allows us to first study the
p
-torsion of the Greenberg Selmer groups, which recovers
[
CGLS22
, Lemma 1.2.4]
for arbitrary characters (their assumption
\vartheta|_{G_{\overline{v}}}\neq 1
is not essential). Indeed, by the snake lemma one has a commutative diagram with exact rows

(Gr,
\vartheta
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ker(r_{\overline{v}}^{{\operatorname{Gr}},0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{Gr}}^{S}}(K,M_{\vartheta}[\mathfrak{p}])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{Gr}}^{S}}(K,M_{\vartheta})[\mathfrak{p}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{{H}}^{0}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\vartheta})/\mathfrak{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{{H}}^{1}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\vartheta}[\mathfrak{p}])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{{H}}^{1}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\vartheta})[\mathfrak{p}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{\overline{v}},M_{\vartheta})/\mathfrak{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{\overline{v}},M_{\vartheta}[\mathfrak{p}])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{\overline{v}},M_{\vartheta})[\mathfrak{p}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{\overline{v}}^{{\operatorname{Gr}},0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{\overline{v}}^{{\operatorname{Gr}},1}}

We have to study the kernel and the cokernel of

r_{\overline{v}}^{{\operatorname{Gr}},0}\colon\text{{H}}^{0}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\vartheta})/\mathfrak{p}\to\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{\overline{v}},M_{\vartheta})/\mathfrak{p}.

But its domain and codomain are
0
:
\text{{H}}^{0}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\vartheta})\simeq\text{{H}}^{0}(K^{\Sigma}_{\infty}/K_{\infty},F/\mathcal{O}(\vartheta))
and
\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{\overline{v}},M_{\vartheta})\simeq\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{\overline{v},\infty},F/\mathcal{O}(\vartheta))
, both isomorphisms by Shapiro’s lemma, which are both
\pi
-divisible: If
\vartheta=\mathbf{1}
, they are
F/\mathcal{O}
; and if
\vartheta\neq\mathbf{1}
but
\vartheta|_{G_{\overline{v}}}=\mathbf{1}
, they are
0
and
F/\mathcal{O}
. Otherwise
0=\text{{H}}^{0}(K^{\Sigma}_{\infty}/K_{\infty},\mathbf{F}(\vartheta))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\text{{H}}^{0}(K^{\Sigma}_{\infty}/K_{\infty},F/\mathcal{O})[\mathfrak{p}]
(and similar for the local
\text{{H}}^{0}
) shows that they are torsion
p
-groups with trivial
\pi
-torsion, hence
0
. In particular, the middle row gives the identification
\text{{H}}^{1}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\vartheta}[\mathfrak{p}])=\text{{H}}^{1}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\vartheta})[\mathfrak{p}]
.

We then study the imprimitive Selmer groups with unramified local conditions. There is a similar diagram

(nr,
\vartheta
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ker(r_{\overline{v}}^{{\operatorname{nr}},0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{{\operatorname{nr}}}^{S}}(K,M_{\vartheta}[\mathfrak{p}])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{{\operatorname{nr}}}^{S}}(K,M_{\vartheta})[\mathfrak{p}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{{H}}^{0}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\vartheta})/\mathfrak{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{{H}}^{1}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\vartheta}[\mathfrak{p}])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{{H}}^{1}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\vartheta})[\mathfrak{p}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ker(f_{\vartheta})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{\overline{v}},M_{\vartheta}[\mathfrak{p}])}{\ker({\operatorname{res}}_{M_{\vartheta}[\mathfrak{p}]})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{\overline{v}},M_{\vartheta})}{\ker({\operatorname{res}}_{M_{\vartheta}})}[\mathfrak{p}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{\overline{v}}^{{\operatorname{nr}},0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{\overline{v}}^{{\operatorname{nr}},1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\vartheta}}

where again we need to study the domain and codomain of
r_{\overline{v}}^{{\operatorname{nr}},0}
. But the domain remains the same, so still
\ker(r_{\overline{v}}^{{\operatorname{nr}},0})=0
. We claim that the codomain
\ker(f_{\vartheta})=0
as well.

From here and onward, we use
\tilde{\omega}
(resp.
\tilde{\mathbf{1}}
) to denote a character
\vartheta
of
G_{K}
whose restriction to
G_{\overline{v}}
is
\omega
(resp.
\mathbf{1}
) which may or may not be
\omega
(resp.
\mathbf{1}
) on
G_{K}
.

When
\vartheta|_{G_{\overline{v}}}=\omega
,
proposition

1.1.3
(i) shows that
\ker({\operatorname{res}}_{M_{\tilde{\omega}}})=0
. Actually, the same argument shows that
\ker({\operatorname{res}}_{M_{\tilde{\omega}}[\mathfrak{p}]})=0
as well, since both
M_{\tilde{\omega}}^{G_{\overline{v}}}
and
M_{\tilde{\omega}}[\mathfrak{p}]^{G_{\overline{v}}}
are
0
. Therefore, if
\vartheta|_{G_{\overline{v}}}=\omega
, the above two diagrams agree, so
\ker(f_{\tilde{\omega}})=\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{\overline{v}},M_{\tilde{\omega}})/\mathfrak{p}=0
.

When
\vartheta|_{G_{\overline{v}}}=\mathbf{1}
, however, we need to study the map
f_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}}
in the bottom row more carefully. Note that in general for
C
a
\mathfrak{p}
-divisible submodule of
A
we have an isomorphism

(1.6)

A/C[\mathfrak{p}]\simeq A[\mathfrak{p}]/C[\mathfrak{p}].

Notice that from
proposition

1.1.3
(i),
\ker({\operatorname{res}}_{M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}}})=F/\mathcal{O}
. A similar argument shows that for
M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}}[\mathfrak{p}]
, the exact sequence (
1.2
) reads

0\to\ker({\operatorname{res}}_{M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}}[\mathfrak{p}]})^{\vee}\to\mathbf{F}[\Gamma/I_{w}]\to\mathbf{F}[\Gamma/I_{w}]\to\mathbf{F}\to 0,

so
\ker({\operatorname{res}}_{M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}}[\mathfrak{p}]})=\mathbf{F}
.

Note also that from the last row in the first diagram (
Gr,

ϑ

), since
\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{\overline{v}},M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}})/\mathfrak{p}=0
, there is an identification
\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{\overline{v}},M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}}[\mathfrak{p}])=\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{\overline{v}},M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}})[\mathfrak{p}]
. Therefore the domain of
f_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}}
is identified with
\frac{\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{\overline{v}},M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}})[\mathfrak{p}]}{\ker({\operatorname{res}}_{M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}}[\mathfrak{p}]})}
. Let
A=\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{\overline{v}},M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}})
and
C=\ker({\operatorname{res}}_{M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}}})=F/\mathcal{O}
. The map
f_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}}
then looks like

A[\mathfrak{p}]/\mathbf{F}\xrightarrow{f_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}}}A/(F/\mathcal{O})[\mathfrak{p}]=A[\mathfrak{p}]/(F/\mathcal{O})[\mathfrak{p}]=A[\mathfrak{p}]/\mathbf{F},

where the identification in the middle comes from (
1.6
). However,
f_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}}
is induced by the natural map
\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{\overline{v}},M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}}[\mathfrak{p}])\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{\overline{v}},M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}})[\mathfrak{p}]:=A[\mathfrak{p}]
, so
f_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}}
must itself be an isomorphism on the quotients. Hence
\ker(f_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}})=0
as well.

Hence in both cases
\vartheta|_{G_{\overline{v}}}=\omega
and
\mathbf{1}
,
\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}^{S}}(K,M_{\vartheta}[\mathfrak{p}])\to\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}^{S}}(K,M_{\vartheta})[\mathfrak{p}]
is an isomorphism by the snake lemma applied to the second diagram.
∎

Proposition 1.2.5
.

The
S
-imprimitive Selmer group
\mathfrak{X}_{\vartheta}^{S}
is
\Lambda
-torsion with
\mu
-invariant
0
and
\lambda
-invariant

\lambda(\mathfrak{X}_{\vartheta}^{S})=\lambda(\mathfrak{X}_{\vartheta})+\sum_{w\in\Sigma,w\nmid p}\lambda(\mathcal{P}_{w}(\vartheta)).

Moreover,
\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}^{S}}(K,M_{\vartheta}[\mathfrak{p}])
is finite and if
\vartheta|_{G_{\overline{v}}}\neq\omega
,
{\operatorname{dim}}_{\mathbf{F}}\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}^{S}}(K,M_{\vartheta}[\mathfrak{p}])=\lambda(\mathfrak{X}_{\vartheta}^{S})
.

Proof.

When
\vartheta|_{G_{\overline{v}}}\neq\mathbf{1},\omega
, this is
[
CGLS22
, Proposition 1.2.5]
. Now we obtain from
remark

1.2.3
that the restriction map defining
\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}}(K,M_{\vartheta})
using unramified local conditions is surjective, so the sequence

(1.7)

0\to\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}}(K,M_{\vartheta})\to\text{{H}}^{1}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\vartheta})\to\prod_{w\in\Sigma,w\nmid p}\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},M_{\vartheta})\times\text{{H}}^{1}(I_{\overline{v}},M_{\vartheta})^{G_{\overline{v}}/I_{\overline{v}}}\to 0

is exact. From the definitions, this readily yields the exact sequence

(1.8)

0\to\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}}(K,M_{\vartheta})\to\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}^{S}}(K,M_{\vartheta})\to\prod_{w\in S}\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{w},M_{\vartheta})\to 0,

which combined with
theorem

1.2.2
and
lemma

1.1.1
gives the first part of the proposition.

Essentially the same proof in
loc. cit.
shows
\text{{H}}^{2}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\vartheta})
is 0. More precisely, we adopt the arguments from Greenberg in
[
Gre89
]
, as we now explain.

First note that because
{\operatorname{Gal}}(K^{\Sigma}/K)
has
p
-cohomological dimension
2
,
\text{{H}}^{2}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\vartheta})
is cofree. Proposition 3 in
op. cit.
yields the following equation of
\Lambda
-coranks:

{\operatorname{corank}}_{\Lambda}(\text{{H}}^{1}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\vartheta}))-{\operatorname{corank}}_{\Lambda}(\text{{H}}^{2}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\vartheta}))=1.

Since
{\operatorname{corank}}_{\Lambda}(\text{{H}}^{1}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\vartheta}))\leq 1
(it follows from the exact sequence (
1.7
), together with
theorem

1.2.2
,
lemma

1.1.1
and
proposition

1.1.3
), this gives the cotorsionness of
\text{{H}}^{2}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\vartheta})
, so
\text{{H}}^{2}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\vartheta})=0
.

Then
[
Gre89
, Proposition 5]
shows that
\text{{H}}^{1}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\tilde{{\mathbf{1}}}})^{\vee}
has no finite
\Lambda
-submodules. From
proposition

1.1.3
, we see that
P^{S}:=(\frac{\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{\overline{v}},M_{\tilde{{\mathbf{1}}}})}{\ker({\operatorname{res}}_{{M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}}}})})^{\vee}
fits into an exact sequence

0\to P^{S}\to\Lambda\to\mathcal{O}\to 0,

so
P^{S}
is a free
\Lambda
-module of rank
1
. Then (
1.7
) and (
1.8
) readily yields

\mathfrak{X}^{S}_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}}\cong\frac{\text{{H}}^{1}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}})^{\vee}}{P^{S}}

as
\Lambda
-modules, and by
[
GV00
, Lemma 2.6]
we conclude that
\mathfrak{X}^{S}_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}}
has no finite
\Lambda
-submodules.

Since
\mathfrak{X}_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}}^{S}
is torsion with
\mu
-invariant
0
by
theorem

1.2.2
, the finiteness of
\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}^{S}}(K,M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}})[\mathfrak{p}]
(and hence of
\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}^{S}}(K,M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}}[\mathfrak{p}])
by
lemma

1.2.4
) follows from the structure theorem. It then follows that
\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}^{S}}(K,M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}})
is divisible, and in particular

\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}^{S}}(K,M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}})\cong(F/\mathcal{O})^{\lambda(\mathfrak{X}_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}}^{S})},

which together with
lemma

1.2.4
gives the formula for
\lambda
-invariant.

The same argument shows that
\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}^{S}}(K,M_{\tilde{\omega}}[\mathfrak{p}])
is finite, except that
\mathfrak{X}^{S}_{\tilde{\omega}}
might have some finite submodules (also from
proposition

1.1.3
) so we don’t obtain a simple formula about the
\lambda
-invariant. The computation will be postponed to
section

1.4
.
∎

Corollary 1.2.6
.

(i)

Assume
\vartheta|_{G_{\overline{v}}}\neq\omega
. Then
\text{{H}}^{2}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\vartheta}[\mathfrak{p}])=0
.

(ii)

When
\vartheta|_{G_{\overline{v}}}=\mathbf{1}
, one has an exact sequence

0\to\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{{\operatorname{nr}}}^{S}}(K,M_{\mathbf{1}}[\mathfrak{p}])\to\text{{H}}^{1}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\mathbf{1}}[\mathfrak{p}])\to\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{\overline{v}},M_{\mathbf{1}}[\mathfrak{p}])/\ker({\operatorname{res}}_{M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}}[\mathfrak{p}]})\to 0.

Proof.

(i) When
\vartheta|_{G_{\overline{v}}}\neq\mathbf{1},\omega
, this is proved in
[
CGLS22
]
. Assume
\vartheta|_{G_{\overline{v}}}=\mathbf{1}
.

The cohomology long exact sequence induced by multiplication by
p
on
M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}}
yields an isomorphism

(1.9)

\frac{\text{{H}}^{1}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}})}{p\text{{H}}^{1}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}})}\cong\text{{H}}^{2}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}}[\mathfrak{p}]).

On the other hand, from the exactness of (
1.7
) we deduce the exact sequence

(1.10)

0\to\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}^{S}}(K,M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}})\to\text{{H}}^{1}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}})\to\text{{H}}^{1}(I_{\overline{v}},M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}})^{G_{\overline{v}}/I_{\overline{v}}}\to 0.

Since
\text{{H}}^{1}(I_{\overline{v}},M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}})^{G_{\overline{v}}/I_{\overline{v}}}
is a quotient object of
\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{\overline{v}},M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}})
, the dual of
\text{{H}}^{1}(I_{\overline{v}},M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}})^{G_{\overline{v}}/I_{\overline{v}}}
is a subobject of
\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{\overline{v}},M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}})^{\vee}
. By
proposition

1.1.3
(ii) the latter is free of
\Lambda
-rank 1, so the dual of
\text{{H}}^{1}(I_{\overline{v}},M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}})^{G_{\overline{v}}/I_{\overline{v}}}
has no
p
-torsion, too. Since we showed in
proposition

1.2.5
that
\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{nr}}^{S}}(K,M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}})
is divisible, it follows from (
1.10
) that
\text{{H}}^{1}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}})^{\vee}
has no
p
-torsion, and so

\text{{H}}^{2}(K^{\Sigma}/K,M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}}[\mathfrak{p}])\cong 0

by (
1.9
).

(ii) By
remark

1.2.3
(ii), the global to local map defining the imprimitive unramified Selmer group
\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}^{S}_{{\operatorname{nr}}}}(K,M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}})
is surjective. By
proposition

1.2.5
,
\text{{H}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}^{S}_{{\operatorname{nr}}}}(K,M_{\tilde{\mathbf{1}}})
is divisible. Now with our
lemma

1.2.4
, the proof of the second claim in Corollary 1.2.6 in
op. cit.
applies almost
verbatim
to our case. This finishes the proof.
∎

1.3.
Local cohomology groups of
\rho_{f}

Recall that
f\in S_{k}(\Gamma_{0}(N))^{{\operatorname{new}}}
is a newform of weight
k=2r
.
Let
T\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}T_{f}
be the self-dual twist of the
\mathfrak{p}
-adic Galois representation of
f
,
V\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}V_{f}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}T_{f}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}F
, and
W\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}W_{f}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}V_{f}/T_{f}
.
Let
M_{f}
be the
G_{K}
-module

M_{f}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}T\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\Lambda^{\vee}.

Note that one has an exact sequence

0\to W_{f}\to M_{f}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot T}}{{\to}}M_{f}\to 0.

Recall that by
\tilde{\mathbf{1}}
(resp.
\tilde{\omega}
) we mean a character
\vartheta
of
G_{K}
with
\vartheta|_{G_{p}}=\mathbf{1}
(resp.
\omega
) which may or may not be
\mathbf{1}
(resp.
\omega
) on
G_{K}
. Compared to
[
CGLS22
]
, we then have two new cases to deal with:

(i)

0\to\mathbf{F}(\tilde{\mathbf{1}})\to\overline{\rho}_{f}\to\mathbf{F}(\tilde{\omega})\to 0

(ii)

0\to\mathbf{F}(\tilde{\omega})\to\overline{\rho}_{f}\to\mathbf{F}(\tilde{\mathbf{1}})\to 0

We will see in section
1.4
that the choice of the ordering of the characters appearing in the decomposition in
\overline{\rho}_{f}
will not matter. For our convenience, assume we are in case (ii). This assumption is in effect throughout section
1.3
and section
1.4
.

Proposition 1.3.1
.

Let
\rho
be an irreducible
2
-dimensional
p
-adic Galois representation whose residual representation
\overline{\rho}
has semisimplification the direct sum of two
1
-dimensional characters. Then there is a lattice such that the associated Galois representation is a non-split extension of those two characters in a given ordering.

Proof.

See
[
Bel09
, Proposition 1.4]

∎

Note that
V_{f}
is an irreducible
G_{K}
-module (this follows from the Tate conjecture for abelian varieties if
k=2
and in for newforms of general weight from
[
Rib77
, Theorem 2.3]
), but becomes reducible when restricted to
G_{K_{v}}
because it is crystalline with distinct Hodge–Tate weights and characteristic polynomial having a unit root
[
BC09
, Theorem 8.3.6]
. Hence by isogeny invariance of the BSD Conjecture, we can assume

\overline{\rho}_{f}|_{G_{K}}
non-split with no trivial subrepresentation and that there is a short exact sequence

0\to\mathbf{F}(\widetilde{\varphi})\to\overline{\rho}_{f}|_{G_{K}}\to\mathbf{F}(\widetilde{\psi})\to 0

with
\varphi=\widetilde{\varphi}|_{G_{K_{v}}}=\omega
.

Theorem 1.3.2
.

The
p
-adic Galois representation
\rho_{f}|_{G_{K_{w}}}
is crystalline with Hodge–Tate weights
\{r,1-r\}
.

Proof.

This is because
p\nmid N

[
Sch90
, Theorem 1.2.4 (ii)]
(note that the condition
p\geq k
there can be removed by
[
Tsu99
]
). It has Hodge–Tate weights
\{r,1-r\}
since we have Hodge–Tate weights
\{0,k-1\}
before taking the self-dual twist by
1-r

[
Sch90
, 4.2.0]
.
∎

We first compute some
\text{{H}}^{0}
cohomology groups.

Proposition 1.3.3
.

Let
p>2
be a prime. Let
K/\mathbf{Q}
be an imaginary quadratic field with
p=v\overline{v}
split in
K
and
K_{\infty}|K
be the anticyclotomic
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension.

Assume the reduction
\overline{\rho}_{f}=V_{f}/T_{f}[\mathfrak{p}]
of
V_{f}
a
2
-dimensional residual Galois representation of
G_{\mathbf{Q}}
which is a non-split extension

0\to\mathbf{F}(\varphi)\to\overline{\rho}_{f}\to\mathbf{F}(\psi)\to 0

with
\varphi=\varepsilon\omega^{k-1}
and
\psi=\varepsilon^{-1}
and
\varepsilon
unramified at
p
. Then
\text{{H}}^{0}(G_{K_{\infty}},\overline{\rho}_{f})=0
(i.e.,
\overline{\rho}_{f}
remains non-split over
G_{K_{\infty}}
).

Proof.

Since
\overline{\rho}_{f}
is non-split, it defines a nonzero extension class in

{\operatorname{Ext}}_{\mathbf{F}[G_{K}]}^{1}(\mathbf{F}(\varepsilon^{-1}),\mathbf{F}(\varepsilon\omega^{k-1}))\simeq{\operatorname{Ext}}_{\mathbf{F}[G_{K}]}^{1}(\mathbf{F},\mathbf{F}(\varepsilon^{2}\omega^{k-1}))\simeq\text{{H}}^{1}(K,\mathbf{F}(\varepsilon^{2}\omega^{k-1}));

here we use that twisting by
\varepsilon
induces an isomorphism on
{\operatorname{Ext}}^{1}
. Thus it suffices to show that
{\operatorname{res}}\colon\text{{H}}^{1}(K,\mathbf{F}(\varepsilon^{2}\omega^{k-1}))\to\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{\infty},\mathbf{F}(\varepsilon^{2}\omega^{k-1}))
is injective.

Let
L=K(\varepsilon^{2})
. It is an extension of
K
of degree dividing
(\#\mathbf{F}-1)/2
, hence coprime to
p
. It is also unramified at
p
. Since
K(\omega^{k-1})/K
is totally ramified at
p
as
p>2
and
K\neq\mathbf{Q}(\sqrt{-3}),\mathbf{Q}(\sqrt{-1})
, one has
K(\omega)\cap L=K
. Note that
K(\omega^{k-1})\supsetneq K
because otherwise
2\mid p-1={\operatorname{ord}}(\omega)\mid(k-1)
, but
k
is even.

Now consider the commutative diagram of inflation-restriction sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{\infty}|K,\mathbf{F}(\varepsilon^{2}\omega^{k-1})^{G_{K_{\infty}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{{H}}^{1}(K,\mathbf{F}(\varepsilon^{2}\omega^{k-1}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{{H}}^{1}(K_{\infty},\mathbf{F}(\varepsilon^{2}\omega^{k-1}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{{H}}^{1}(LK_{\infty}|L,\mathbf{F}(\varepsilon^{2}\omega^{k-1})^{G_{LK_{\infty}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{{H}}^{1}(L,\mathbf{F}(\varepsilon^{2}\omega^{k-1}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{{H}}^{1}(LK_{\infty},\mathbf{F}(\varepsilon^{2}\omega^{k-1}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{inf}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{res}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{inf}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{res}}}

The middle vertical arrow is injective by restriction-corestriction because
p\nmid[L:K]
. The lower left group is
0
because
\mathbf{F}(\varepsilon^{2}\omega^{k-1})^{G_{LK_{\infty}}}=\mathbf{F}(\omega^{k-1})^{G_{LK_{\infty}}}=0
since no non-trivial
p
-th root of unity is contained in
K_{\infty}
because it is a
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension of
K
and
\mu_{p}(K)=\{1\}
since
p>2
and
K\neq\mathbf{Q}(\sqrt{-3}),\mathbf{Q}(\sqrt{-1})
; note that
\omega^{k-1}\neq\mathbf{1}
. Hence the lower restriction morphism is injective, hence by commutativity and exactness of the diagram the top restriction is injective.
∎

For simplicity, in the local cohomology groups, we write the characters as
\mathbf{1}
and
\omega
instead of
\tilde{\mathbf{1}}
and
\tilde{\omega}
since we do not care about their global behavior.

Proposition 1.3.4
.

Let
w\mid p
. Assume the Galois representation
\overline{\rho}_{f}
does not have a trivial subrepresentation. Assume that for
w\mid p
,
\overline{\rho}_{f}|_{G_{K_{w}}}
is an extension of the trivial representation
\mathbf{F}
by
\mathbf{F}(\omega)
.

(i)

\text{{H}}^{0}(K,V_{f}/T_{f})=0
.

(ii)

\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{w},V_{f}/T_{f})
is finite and cyclic, i.e., isomorphic to
\mathcal{O}/\mathfrak{p}^{a}
with
a\geq 0
.

(iii)

\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{w},M_{f})
is finite and cyclic, i.e., isomorphic to
\mathcal{O}/\mathfrak{p}^{b}
with
b\geq 0
.

Proof.

(i)

Follows by assumption on
T_{f}
by
proposition

1.3.1
.

(ii)

Note that we have
V_{f}/T_{f}=M_{f}[T]
, hence
\text{{H}}^{0}(?,V_{f}/T_{f})\subseteq\text{{H}}^{0}(?_{\infty},V_{f}/T_{f})[T]
for
?\in\{K,K_{w}\}
, so (ii) follows from (iii).

(iii)

Note that
\rho_{f}|_{G_{K_{w}}}
is crystalline with Hodge–Tate weights
\{r,1-r\}
by
theorem

1.3.2
.
By
[
BC09
, Theorem 8.3.6]
,
\rho_{f}|_{G_{w}}
is reducible

0\to F(\sigma)\to\rho_{f}|_{G_{w}}\to F(\tau)\to 0

with characters
\sigma,\tau
having Hodge–Tate weights
\{r,1-r\}
. By
[
BC09
, Proposition 8.3.4]
,
\{\sigma,\tau\}=\{\varepsilon\chi^{r},\varepsilon^{-1}\chi^{1-r}\}
with
\varepsilon
an unramified character of
G_{K_{w}}
. But for
m\neq 0
,
\varepsilon\chi^{m}
is non-trivial on
G_{K_{\infty,w}}
by
lemma

1.0.2
(ii). Hence
\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{\infty,w},F(\varepsilon\chi^{m}))=0
, so
\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{\infty,w},F/\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon\chi^{m}))\subseteq F/\mathcal{O}
is finite and cyclic whenever
r>1
. (Note that
\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{\infty,w},F(\varepsilon\chi^{m}))=0
implies
\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{\infty,w},F/\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon\chi^{m}))
is finite by
[
KO20
, Lemma 2.7]
). Hence in order to show
\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{\infty,w},V_{f}/T_{f})
is cyclic, it remains to show that for one of
?=\varphi,\psi
, the invariants
\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{\infty,w},F/\mathcal{O}(?))=0
.

Note that at least one
\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{w},\mathbf{F}(\varphi)),\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{w},\mathbf{F}(\psi))
is
0
(since one of them is
\omega
when restricted to
G_{w}
). Since
{\operatorname{ord}}(\omega|_{G_{K_{\infty}}})=p-1
(since
p-1
is coprime to
p
), also at least one
\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{\infty,w},F/\mathcal{O}(\varphi)),\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{\infty,w},F/\mathcal{O}(\psi))
is
0
. Hence
\text{{H}}^{0}(K_{\infty,w},V_{f}/T_{f})\subseteq F/\mathcal{O}
and the finiteness result in the above paragraph shows that we cannot have equality.

Now if
r=1
,
\rho_{f}
is the
\mathfrak{p}
-adic Galois representation attached to an abelian variety
A/K_{w}
with good reduction. Since
